This section shall not apply to those employees who have been adjudicated permanently totally disabled, or to those employees who have been administratively determined pursuant to division rules to be permanently totally disabled.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.239.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.66.0">1987 c 332 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/89/#laws.0.22.0">2011 c 89 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/51/#laws.2.6.0">2023 c 51 art 2 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.27.0">2024 c 97 s 27</a>-34</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.24"><b>176.24</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.241"><b>176.241</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.117.0">1987 c 332 s 117</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.242"><b>176.242</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.117.0">1987 c 332 s 117</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.2421"><b>176.2421</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.117.0">1987 c 332 s 117</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.243"><b>176.243</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.117.0">1987 c 332 s 117</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.244"><b>176.244</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.117.0">1987 c 332 s 117</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RECEIPTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.245">
        <h1 class="shn">176.245 RECEIPTS FOR PAYMENT OF COMPENSATION, FILING.</h1>
        <p>An employer shall promptly file with the division receipts for payment of compensation as may be required by the rules of the division.</p>
        <p>The commissioner of the Department of Labor and Industry shall periodically check its records to determine whether these receipts have been promptly filed, and if not, shall require the employer to do so. The commissioner may determine the most efficient manner of reviewing or auditing the records filed under this chapter, including using sampling methodology, to determine compliance with this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.245.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.35.0">1953 c 755 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.80.0">1973 c 388 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/250/#laws.0.13.0">2008 c 250 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/70/#laws.1.6.0">2013 c 70 art 1 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.25"><b>176.25</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DUTIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.251">
        <h1 class="shn">176.251 DUTIES OF COMMISSIONER OF DEPARTMENT OF LABOR AND INDUSTRY.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of the Department of Labor and Industry shall actually supervise and require prompt and full compliance with all provisions of this chapter relating to the payment of compensation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.251.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.36.0">1953 c 755 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.81.0">1973 c 388 s 81</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.253">
        <h1 class="shn">176.253 INSURER, EMPLOYER, AND THIRD-PARTY ADMINISTRATOR; PERFORMANCE OF ACTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.253.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The terms used in this section have the meanings given to them in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) "Department" has the meaning in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.011#stat.176.011.8b">176.011, subdivision 8b</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) "Employer" means an employer as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.011#stat.176.011.10">176.011, subdivision 10</a>, against whom a workers' compensation claim has been asserted or who is liable for a workers' compensation injury under this chapter. Employer includes:</p>
          <p>(1) an employer authorized to self-insure by the Department of Commerce under chapter 79A; and</p>
          <p>(2) the state or a political subdivision that is not required to be authorized to self-insure by the commissioner of commerce in order to pay its workers' compensation claims.</p>
          <p>(d) "Insurer" means a workers' compensation insurer licensed by the Department of Commerce under section 60A.</p>
          <p>(e) "Third-party administrator" means an administrator that is licensed by the Department of Commerce to administer a workers' compensation self-insurance or insurance plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/60A.23#stat.60A.23.8">60A.23, subdivision 8</a>, with a contract to act on behalf of an employer or insurer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.253.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>Where this chapter requires an employer to perform an act, the insurer of the employer may perform that act. Where the insurer acts on behalf of the employer, the employer is responsible for the authorized acts of the insurer and for any delay, failure, or refusal of the insurer to perform the act. This section does not relieve the employer from any penalty or forfeiture which this chapter imposes on the employer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.253.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Authority of third-party administrator.</span></h2>
          <p>A third-party administrator that has an active account in CAMPUS under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.2612">176.2612</a> may act on behalf of the employer or insurer as provided in the contract between the administrator and the employer or insurer. If the department or commissioner issues an order or assesses a penalty against an employer or insurer, the order or penalty must be served on any administrator acting on behalf of the employer or insurer. A third-party administrator has the authority to act on behalf of the employer or insurer in responding to a commissioner or department inquiry, order or penalty assessment, or paying a penalty, until the insurer or administrator notifies the department in writing that the administrator does not have authority.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.253.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.37.0">1953 c 755 s 37</a>; 1986 c 444; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/7/#laws.12.7.0">1Sp2019 c 7 art 12 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.35.0">2024 c 97 s 35</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.255"><b>176.255</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.26"><b>176.26</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.261">
        <h1 class="shn">176.261 EMPLOYEE OF COMMISSIONER OF DEPARTMENT OF LABOR AND INDUSTRY MAY ACT FOR AND ADVISE A PARTY TO A PROCEEDING.</h1>
        <p>When requested by an employer or an employee or an employee's dependent, the commissioner of the Department of Labor and Industry may designate one or more of the division employees to advise that party of rights under this chapter, and as far as possible to assist in adjusting differences between the parties. The person so designated may appear in person in any proceedings under this chapter as the representative or adviser of the party. In such case, the party need not be represented by an attorney at law.</p>
        <p>Prior to advising an employee or employer to seek assistance outside of the department, the department must refer employers and employees seeking advice or requesting assistance in resolving a dispute to an attorney or other technical, paraprofessional, or professional Workers' Compensation Division employee, whichever is appropriate.</p>
        <p>The department must make efforts to settle problems of employees and employers by contacting third parties, including attorneys, insurers, and health care providers, on behalf of employers and employees and using the department's persuasion to settle issues quickly and cooperatively. The obligation to make efforts to settle problems exists whether or not a formal claim has been filed with the department.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.261.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.38.0">1953 c 755 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.82.0">1973 c 388 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/510/#laws.3.29.0">1992 c 510 art 3 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/231/#laws.2.92.0">1995 c 231 art 2 s 92</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/374/#laws.0.7.0">1996 c 374 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.2611">
        <h1 class="shn">176.2611 COORDINATION OF THE OFFICE OF ADMINISTRATIVE HEARINGS' CASE MANAGEMENT SYSTEM AND THE WORKERS' COMPENSATION IMAGING SYSTEM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2611.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the definitions in this subdivision apply unless otherwise specified.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of labor and industry.</p>
          <p>(c) "Department" means the Department of Labor and Industry.</p>
          <p>(d) "Document" includes all data, whether in electronic or paper format, that is filed with or issued by the office or department related to a claim-specific dispute resolution proceeding under this section.</p>
          <p>(e) "Office" means the Office of Administrative Hearings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2611.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>Subject to further amendments pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.2612">176.2612</a>, subdivision 2, this section specifies whether documents must be filed with the office or the commissioner, and governs access to dispute-related documents and data at the office or department. This section prevails over any conflicting provision in this chapter, Laws 1998, chapter 366, or corresponding rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2611.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Documents that must be filed with the office.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 4 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.421">176.421</a>, all documents that require action by the office under this chapter must be filed, electronically or in paper format, with the office as required by the chief administrative law judge. Filing a document that initiates or is filed in preparation for a proceeding at the office satisfies any requirement under this chapter that the document must be filed with the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2611.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Documents that must be filed with the commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following documents must be filed directly with the commissioner in the format and manner prescribed by the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) all requests for an administrative conference under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106">176.106</a>, regardless of the amount in dispute;</p>
          <p>(2) a motion to intervene in an administrative conference that is pending at the department;</p>
          <p>(3) any other document related to an administrative conference that is pending at the department;</p>
          <p>(4) an objection to a penalty assessed by the commissioner or the department;</p>
          <p>(5) requests for medical and rehabilitation dispute certification under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.081">176.081</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (c), including related documents; and</p>
          <p>(6) except as provided in this subdivision or subdivision 3, any other document required to be filed with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The filing requirement in paragraph (a), clause (1), makes no changes to the jurisdictional provisions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106">176.106</a>. A claim petition that contains only medical or rehabilitation issues, unless primary liability is disputed, is considered to be a request for an administrative conference and must be filed with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must refer a timely, unresolved objection to a penalty under paragraph (a), clause (4), to the office within 60 calendar days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2611.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Form revision and access to documents and data.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must revise dispute resolution forms, in consultation with the chief administrative law judge, to reflect the filing requirements in this section.</p>
          <p>(b) Until August 31, 2020, the office must send the department all documents that are accepted for filing or issued by the office. The office must send the documents to the department, electronically or by courier, within two business days of when the documents are accepted for filing or issued by the office. Beginning August 31, 2020, all dispute-related documents accepted for filing or issued by the office, and all dispute-related documents filed with the department that are referred to the office under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106">176.106</a>, must be immediately transmitted between the office's case management system and CAMPUS using application programming interfaces.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2611.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Data privacy.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All documents filed with or issued by the office under this chapter are private data on individuals and nonpublic data pursuant to chapter 13, except that the documents are available to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the office;</p>
          <p>(2) the department;</p>
          <p>(3) the employer;</p>
          <p>(4) the insurer;</p>
          <p>(5) the employee;</p>
          <p>(6) the dependent of a deceased employee;</p>
          <p>(7) an intervenor in the dispute;</p>
          <p>(8) the attorney to a party in the dispute;</p>
          <p>(9) a person who furnishes written authorization from the employer, insurer, employee, or dependent of a deceased employee; and</p>
          <p>(10) a person, agency, or other entity allowed access to the documents under this chapter or other law.</p>
          <p class="bl">Once a document filed with or issued by the office under this chapter is transmitted to the commissioner under subdivision 5 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.281">176.281</a>, access to the document in the division file is as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.231">176.231</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The office and department may post notice of scheduled proceedings on the agencies' websites and at their principal places of business in any manner that protects the employee's identifying information. Identifying information includes the employee's name or any part of the employee's name.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2611.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals.</span></h2>
          <p>The Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals has authority to amend its rules of procedure to reflect electronic filing with the office under this section for purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.421#stat.176.421.5">176.421, subdivision 5</a>, and to allow electronic filing with the court under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.285">176.285</a>. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.125">14.125</a> does not apply to the agency's rulemaking authority under this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.281">176.281</a>, paragraph (d), and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.285">176.285</a>, subdivision 2a.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.2611.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/185/#laws.1.4.0">2018 c 185 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/7/#laws.12.8.0">1Sp2019 c 7 art 12 s 8</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/7/1/#laws.2.17.0">7Sp2020 c 1 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.36.0">2024 c 97 s 36</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.2612">
        <h1 class="shn">176.2612 WORKERS' COMPENSATION CLAIMS ACCESS AND MANAGEMENT PLATFORM USER SYSTEM (CAMPUS).</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2612.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall maintain the workers' compensation Claims Access and Management Platform User System (CAMPUS) as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.011#stat.176.011.1d">176.011, subdivision 1d</a>. This section applies to the department and the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals. Except for paragraph (b), clause (4), this subdivision does not apply to the office.</p>
          <p>(b) CAMPUS must:</p>
          <p>(1) provide a single filing system for users to electronically file documents required or authorized to be filed under this chapter with the commissioner or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals;</p>
          <p>(2) maintain and retain the division file and other claim-related documents;</p>
          <p>(3) accept filings by electronic data entry and by uploaded images of supplemental documents, such as a medical or narrative report or document;</p>
          <p>(4) electronically and securely transmit data, and images of documents, between each agency to allow the agency to perform its statutory functions;</p>
          <p>(5) electronically and securely serve documents;</p>
          <p>(6) organize electronic data filed in the division file into an image for viewing or printing by parties to a claim and staff at each agency;</p>
          <p>(7) provide electronic access to the division file by parties and each agency to workers' compensation documents and other data as authorized or required by this chapter and generate an audit trail when the division file is accessed by a person; and</p>
          <p>(8) allow authorized stakeholders, the department, and the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals to manage and monitor claims and perform statutorily required functions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2612.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Plan and proposal for improvement.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 11, 2021, the commissioner must recommend to the Workers' Compensation Advisory Council a plan and proposed statutory amendments for the most effective means, based on an assessment of benefits and value, to implement improvements to CAMPUS and the case management system at the office, including ensuring a single calendaring system and a single filing system. The filing requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.2611">176.2611, subdivisions 3</a> and 4, remain in effect until further amendments related to a single filing system in CAMPUS are enacted pursuant to the recommendations of the Workers' Compensation Advisory Council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.2612.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Creating a CAMPUS account.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "employer," "insurer," and "third-party administrator" have the meanings given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.253#stat.176.253.1">176.253, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Electronic access to view or file documents in CAMPUS shall be granted according to the requirements established by the department and the Department of Information Technology Services to authenticate the identity of the person or entity creating the account and authorize access to the documents that the person or entity is entitled to under this chapter. To create an account in CAMPUS, a person must provide the commissioner of labor and industry with information needed to create the account and authenticate the person's identity. The person must also agree to terms and conditions that are needed to safeguard the security and privacy of data and comply with the requirements of this chapter related to CAMPUS.</p>
          <p>(c) The persons or entities in clauses (1) to (12) must create and maintain an account in CAMPUS to electronically access or file documents:</p>
          <p>(1) an employee with a workers' compensation claim or other person who has access to the division file under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.231#stat.176.231.9">176.231, subdivision 9</a>, paragraph (c);</p>
          <p>(2) an employer with a workers' compensation claim;</p>
          <p>(3) a licensed workers' compensation insurer acting on behalf of an employer with a Minnesota workers' compensation claim;</p>
          <p>(4) an intervenor or potential intervenor in a workers' compensation dispute;</p>
          <p>(5) a registered rehabilitation provider under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.102">176.102</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) the state or a political subdivision or school district that is not required to be self-insured by the commissioner of the Department of Commerce in order to pay its workers' compensation claims;</p>
          <p>(7) the assigned risk plan under chapter 79A;</p>
          <p>(8) the Workers' Compensation Reinsurance Association under chapter 79;</p>
          <p>(9) the Minnesota insurance guaranty association established under chapter 60C;</p>
          <p>(10) the self-insurers' security fund under chapter 79A;</p>
          <p>(11) a third-party administrator that has contracted to act on behalf of any of the entities listed in this subdivision; and</p>
          <p>(12) an attorney representing a person or entity listed above.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may require that any person or entity listed in paragraph (c), clauses (2) to (12), create and maintain an account in CAMPUS if the person or entity is a party to a workers' compensation claim or associated with an enforcement action of the department.</p>
          <p>(e) A designated medical contact under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.135">176.135</a> and a managed care organization certified by the department under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.1351">176.1351</a> must create and maintain an account to file and view documents related to the certified managed care plan or designated medical contact. A program administrator for a collective bargaining agreement approved by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.1812">176.1812</a> must create an account to view documents related to a claim that is covered by the agreement. A health care provider must create an account to file a request for an administrative conference if permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.136#stat.176.136.2">176.136, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p>(f) If a person or entity is required to create and maintain an account under this subdivision and fails to do so:</p>
          <p>(1) unless good cause is shown, the commissioner may assess a $500 penalty against the person or entity for each 30-day period that an account is not created or maintained following the commissioner's notice that one is required;</p>
          <p>(2) failure to create or maintain an account shall not be a defense to untimely filing where electronic filing is required under this chapter; and</p>
          <p>(3) failure to create or maintain an account results in the appointment of the commissioner and successors in office as the person's agent to receive service by the commissioner or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals where service is required under this chapter, provided that the commissioner attempts service by United States mail on the party at the last known address.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.2612.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/7/#laws.12.11.0">1Sp2019 c 7 art 12 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/7/1/#laws.2.18.0">7Sp2020 c 1 art 2 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.2.16.0">2021 c 31 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.1.3.0">2022 c 32 art 1 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.2615"><b>176.2615</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/182/#laws.0.8.0">2014 c 182 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.262"><b>176.262</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.173.0">1983 c 290 s 173</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.265"><b>176.265</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/461/#laws.0.37.0">1986 c 461 s 37</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.27"><b>176.27</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PROCEDURE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.271">
        <h1 class="shn">176.271 INITIATION OF PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.271.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Written petition.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided by this chapter or by the commissioner, all proceedings under this chapter are initiated by the filing of a written petition on a prescribed form with the department or office. All claim petitions shall include the information required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.291">176.291</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.271.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.117.0">1987 c 332 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.271.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.40.0">1953 c 755 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.84.0">1973 c 388 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/1/3/#laws.0.58.0">Ex1979 c 3 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/432/#laws.2.43.0">1984 c 432 art 2 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.67.0">1987 c 332 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.37.0">2024 c 97 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.275">
        <h1 class="shn">176.275 FILING OF PAPERS; PROOF OF SERVICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.275.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Filing.</span></h2>
          <p>If a document is required to be filed by this chapter or any rules adopted pursuant to authority granted by this chapter, the filing shall be completed upon acceptance of the document by the agency. Any document that lacks information required by statute or rule, or is not filed in the manner and format required by this chapter, may be rejected. A document rejected for any of these reasons is not considered filed. An agency is not required to maintain, and may destroy, a duplicate of a document that has already been filed. If a workers' compensation identification number has been assigned by the department, it must be substituted for the Social Security number on a document. The commissioner may request additional proof of an injured worker's identity before assigning an identification number.</p>
          <p>A notice or other document required to be served or filed at either the department, the office, or the court of appeals which is inadvertently served or filed at the wrong one of these agencies by an unrepresented employee shall be deemed to have been served or filed with the proper agency. The receiving agency shall note the date of receipt of a document and shall forward the documents to the proper agency no later than two working days following receipt.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.275.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Proof of service; affidavits and notarized statements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Whenever a provision of this chapter or rules adopted pursuant to authority granted by this chapter require either a proof of service, an affidavit of service, or a notarized statement on a document, the requirement is satisfied by a document that meets the definition of an affidavit under Rule 15 of the General Rules of Practice for the district courts.</p>
          <p>(b) An agency is not required to verify the accuracy of a proof or affidavit of service filed by a party before accepting a document for filing. This does not prevent a party from asserting insufficient or lack of service in a proceeding.</p>
          <p>(c) Service on a party's attorney constitutes service on the represented party, unless service on the employee is specifically required by this chapter.</p>
          <p>(d) A party is not required to file a proof or affidavit of service of a document on a person when the party uses the agency's electronic system to serve the person.</p>
          <p>(e) A party to a claim who uses an agency's electronic system to (1) improperly file a document that is unrelated to the workers' compensation claim in which the document was filed, or (2) send or serve a document on a recipient who is not entitled to receive it under this chapter must, upon discovery or notification of the improper release, promptly notify the recipient, the agency, and the subject whose data was improperly released. The agency whose electronic system was used to send, serve, or file the document is not responsible under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.971">3.971</a> and chapter 13 for the improper release, but must promptly correct its files or remove the document from its electronic system upon discovery or notification.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.275.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.41.0">1953 c 755 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.85.0">1973 c 388 s 85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.68.0">1987 c 332 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/231/#laws.2.94.0">1995 c 231 art 2 s 94</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/250/#laws.0.14.0">2008 c 250 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.3.4.0">2017 c 94 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/7/#laws.12.12.0">1Sp2019 c 7 art 12 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/7/1/#laws.2.20.0">7Sp2020 c 1 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.38.0">2024 c 97 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.28"><b>176.28</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.281">
        <h1 class="shn">176.281 ORDERS, DECISIONS, AND AWARDS; FILING; SERVICE.</h1>
        <p>(a) When the commissioner or compensation judge or Office of Administrative Hearings or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals has issued correspondence, a notice, order, decision, award, or other disposition or outcome of a dispute, or an amendment to an order, decision, or award, it shall be filed immediately with the commissioner.</p>
        <p>(b) The agencies' systems must be configured so that transmission of data and documents described in paragraph (a) and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.2611#stat.176.2611.5">176.2611, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), are immediately transmitted between the Office of Administrative Hearings case management system and CAMPUS using application programming interfaces.</p>
        <p>(c) If the commissioner, compensation judge, Office of Administrative Hearings, or Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals has rendered a final order, decision, or award, or amendment thereto, the commissioner or the Office of Administrative Hearings or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals shall immediately serve a copy upon every party in interest, together with a notification of the date the order was filed.</p>
        <p>(d) On all orders, decisions, awards, and other documents, the commissioner or compensation judge or Office of Administrative Hearings or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals may digitize the signatures of all officials, including judges, for the use of electronic data interchange and clerical automation. These signatures shall have the same legal authority of an original signature, provided that proper security is used to safeguard the use of the digitized signatures and each digitized signature has been certified by the division, department, office, or court of appeals before its use, in accordance with rules adopted by that agency or court.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.281.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.42.0">1953 c 755 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.86.0">1973 c 388 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/405/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 405 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.142.0">1983 c 290 s 142</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/231/#laws.2.95.0">1995 c 231 art 2 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/7/#laws.12.13.0">1Sp2019 c 7 art 12 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.285">
        <h1 class="shn">176.285 SERVICE OF PAPERS AND NOTICES; ELECTRONIC FILING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.285.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Service by mail.</span></h2>
          <p>Service of documents shall be by first class United States mail or personal service, except where electronic service is authorized or required under this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.275">176.275</a>. An employee cannot be required to accept electronic service where service on the employee is required. Where service is by mail, service is effected at the time mailed if properly addressed and stamped. If it is so mailed, it is presumed the paper or notice reached the party to be served. However, a party may show by competent evidence that that party did not receive it or that it had been delayed in transit for an unusual or unreasonable period of time. In case of nonreceipt or delay, an allowance shall be made for the party's failure to assert a right within the prescribed time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.285.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Electronic service and filing on an agency.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Where a statute or rule authorizes or requires a document to be filed with or served on the office, the document must be filed electronically in the manner and in the format specified by the office. Where a statute or rule authorizes or requires a document to be filed with or served on the commissioner or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, the document must be filed electronically in the manner and format specified by the commissioner. An employee must not be required to file a document electronically at any agency unless the document is filed by an attorney on behalf of the employee.</p>
          <p>(b) If electronic filing of a document is required under this subdivision and a statute or rule requires a copy of the document to be provided or served on another person or party, the document filed electronically with the agency and provided or served on the other person or party must contain the same information in the format required by the agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For purposes of serving on and filing with an agency under this chapter, "electronic" and "electronically" excludes facsimile and email unless authorized by the agency. A document is deemed filed with an agency on the business day it is accepted for filing on or before 11:59 p.m.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.285.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Electronic signatures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Where a statute or rule authorizes or requires a person's signature on a document to be filed with or served on an agency, the signature may be an electronic signature, as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325L.02">325L.02</a>, or transmitted electronically, if authorized by the agency and if the signature is transmitted in the manner and format specified by the agency. The commissioner may require that a document authorized or required to be filed with the commissioner, department, or division be filed electronically in the manner and format specified by the commissioner, except that an employee must not be required to file a document electronically unless the document is filed by an attorney on behalf of an employee. The department, Workers Compensation Court of Appeals, or office may adopt rules for the certification of signatures. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.125">14.125</a> does not apply to the rulemaking authority under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) If a rehabilitation provider files a rehabilitation plan or other document that requires the signature of the employee, employer, or insurer pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.102">176.102</a>, or rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.102">176.102</a>, the rehabilitation provider shall specify whether each party's signature has been obtained. The rehabilitation provider must retain the document with the original signature or signatures of the employee and insurer or self-insured employer for five years after the rehabilitation plan is closed and must make the signed document available to the commissioner or compensation judge upon request.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.285.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Electronic service of documents on party through office case management system or CAMPUS.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The office and a party may electronically serve through the office's case management system a document required to be served on a party or filed with the office on any person with an account in the case management system.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner, the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, and a party may electronically serve through CAMPUS a document required to be served on a party or filed with the commissioner on any person with an account in CAMPUS under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.2612">176.2612</a>. Service through CAMPUS must be either by secure email or by emailing a notice that the document may be accessed through a web portal. Service of a document through CAMPUS on an attorney for a party is considered to be service on the party, except where service on the employee is specifically required by this chapter.</p>
          <p>(c) An employee must not be electronically served unless the employee has created an account and has agreed to accept electronic service through the office's case management system or CAMPUS.</p>
          <p>(d) The date of electronic service of a document is the date the recipient is sent a document electronically, or the date the recipient is notified that the document is available on a website, whichever occurs first.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.285.2c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2c.<span class="headnote">Time to assert right when document served or filed electronically.</span></h2>
          <p>When the electronic filing of a legal document with an agency marks the beginning of a prescribed time for another party to assert a right, the prescribed time for another party to assert a right shall be extended by two calendar days when it can be shown that service to another party was by United States mail, and extended by one business day if the document was electronically served on the party in CAMPUS or the office's case management system after 4:30 p.m.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.285.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Proof of service of documents served by parties and agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner, the chief administrative law judge, and the chief judge of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals shall ensure that proof of service of all papers and notices served by their respective agencies is transmitted to the division file in the manner described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.281">176.281</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) If a document unrelated to a dispute, such as a first report of injury, is required to be filed with the commissioner and required to be served on the employee or other party, the serving party must retain the proof of service and provide it to the commissioner or compensation judge upon request.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.285.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Definitions; applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, "payer" means a workers' compensation insurer, self-insurer employer, or third-party administrator.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as otherwise modified by this chapter, the provisions of chapter 325L apply to electronic signatures and the electronic transmission of documents under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.285.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.43.0">1953 c 755 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.87.0">1973 c 388 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.143.0">1983 c 290 s 143</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/231/#laws.2.96.0">1995 c 231 art 2 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/250/#laws.0.15.0">2008 c 250 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.3.5.0">2017 c 94 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/7/#laws.12.14.0">1Sp2019 c 7 art 12 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/7/1/#laws.2.21.0">7Sp2020 c 1 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.39.0">2024 c 97 s 39</a>-41</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.29"><b>176.29</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.291">
        <h1 class="shn">176.291 DISPUTES; PETITIONS; PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <p>(a) Where there is a dispute as to a question of law or fact in connection with a claim for compensation, a party may serve on all other parties and file a petition with the office stating the matter in dispute. The petition shall be on a form prescribed by the commissioner and shall be signed by the petitioner.</p>
        <p>(b) The petition shall also state and include, where applicable:</p>
        <p>(1) names and residence or business address of parties;</p>
        <p>(2) facts relating to the employment at the time of injury, including amount of wages received;</p>
        <p>(3) extent and character of each injury;</p>
        <p>(4) notice to or knowledge by employer of injury;</p>
        <p>(5) copies of written medical reports or medical records supporting each claim asserted;</p>
        <p>(6) copies of other information in support of the claim;</p>
        <p>(7) names and addresses of all known witnesses intended to be called in support of each injury and claim;</p>
        <p>(8) the desired location of any hearing and estimated time needed to present evidence at the hearing;</p>
        <p>(9) any requests for a prehearing or settlement conference;</p>
        <p>(10) a list of all known third parties, including the Departments of Human Services and Employment and Economic Development, who may have paid any medical bills or other benefits to the employee for the injuries or disease alleged in the petition or for the time the employee was unable to work due to the injuries or disease, together with a listing of the amounts paid by each;</p>
        <p>(11) the nature and extent of each claim; and</p>
        <p>(12) a request for an expedited hearing which must include an attached affidavit of significant financial hardship which complies with the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.341#stat.176.341.6">176.341, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) Incomplete petitions may be stricken or dismissed from the calendar as provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.305#stat.176.305.4">176.305, subdivision 4</a>. Within 14 days of a request by a party, an employee who has filed a claim petition pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.271">176.271</a> or this section shall furnish a list of physicians and health care providers from whom the employee has received treatment for the same or a similar condition as well as authorizations to release relevant information, data, and records to the requester. The petition may be stricken from the calendar upon motion of a party for failure to timely provide the required list of health care providers or authorizations.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.291.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.44.0">1953 c 755 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.88.0">1973 c 388 s 88</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.104.0">1981 c 346 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.69.0">1987 c 332 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/231/#laws.2.97.0">1995 c 231 art 2 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.2.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/51/#laws.2.8.0">2023 c 51 art 2 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.295">
        <h1 class="shn">176.295 NONRESIDENT EMPLOYERS; FOREIGN CORPORATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.295.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Affidavit of inability to effectuate service.</span></h2>
          <p>Where a petitioner, an employee, or an employee's dependent cannot serve a petition for compensation or other notice on an employer because the employer is a nonresident or a foreign corporation, the petitioner may file an affidavit with the chief administrative law judge stating that the petitioner is unable to effectuate service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.295.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action in district court.</span></h2>
          <p>When a petitioner has filed an affidavit of inability to effectuate service pursuant to subdivision 1 with the chief administrative law judge, the petitioner may also file a complaint against the employer in district court. The complaint must be filed in the county in which the employee resided at the time of the injury or death. The complaint shall be commenced and pursued in the same manner as other civil actions in district court. The complaint shall state that a petition for compensation has been filed with the office, and shall be accompanied by a verified copy of the affidavit of inability to effectuate service. The complaint shall also state the facts upon which the right to compensation or other relief is based.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.295.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Attachment, garnishment; service by publication.</span></h2>
          <p>The remedies of attachment and garnishment are available to the petitioner in the district court action. Service of summons may be made by publication.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.295.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">General appearances; security, bond.</span></h2>
          <p>Where the employer makes a general appearance in the district court action and files a bond or security approved by the commissioner of the Department of Labor and Industry, or where an insurer appears generally in the action and assumes liability for any award which may be rendered against the employer, the district court shall dismiss the action.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.295.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.45.0">1953 c 755 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.89.0">1973 c 388 s 89</a>-91; 1986 c 444; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.3.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 3</a>,4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.30"><b>176.30</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.301">
        <h1 class="shn">176.301 DETERMINATION OF ISSUES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.301.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Trial by court; reference to chief administrative law judge.</span></h2>
          <p>When a workers' compensation issue is present in the district court action, the court may try the action itself without a jury, or refer the matter to the chief administrative law judge for assignment to a compensation judge. The compensation judge shall report findings and decisions to the district court. The court may approve or disapprove such decision in the same manner as it approves or disapproves the report of a referee. The court shall enter judgment upon such decision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.301.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appeal from judgment of district court.</span></h2>
          <p>An appeal lies from the judgment of the district court as in other cases.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.301.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.46.0">1953 c 755 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.92.0">1973 c 388 s 92</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.105.0">1981 c 346 s 105</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.70.0">1987 c 332 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.305">
        <h1 class="shn">176.305 PETITIONS FILED WITH WORKERS' COMPENSATION DIVISION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.305.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hearings on petitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The petitioner shall serve a copy of the petition on each adverse party and file a copy with the office, together with an appropriate affidavit of service. Service and filing must be made as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.285">176.285, subdivisions 1</a> and 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.305.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Settlement and pretrial conferences; summary decision.</span></h2>
          <p>The chief administrative law judge shall promptly assign the petition to a compensation judge under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.307">176.307</a>, and shall schedule a settlement conference before a compensation judge, to be held no later than 180 days after a claim petition was filed, or 45 days after a petition to discontinue, objection to discontinuance, or request for formal hearing was filed.</p>
          <p>All parties must appear at the settlement conference, either personally or by representative, must be prepared to discuss settlement of all issues, and must be prepared to discuss or present the information required by the joint rules of the division and the office. If a representative appears on behalf of a party, the representative must have authority to fully settle the matter. The parties shall serve and file a pretrial statement no fewer than five days before the settlement conference.</p>
          <p>If settlement is not reached, the chief administrative law judge shall schedule a hearing to be held within 90 days from the scheduled settlement conference. However, the hearing must be held earlier than 90 days from the scheduled settlement conference if this chapter requires an expedited hearing to be held at an earlier date. The hearing must be held before a compensation judge other than the compensation judge who conducted the settlement conference. The compensation judge assigned to hold the hearing may choose to conduct a pretrial conference to clarify the issues and evidence that will be presented at the hearing.</p>
          <p>Cancellations and continuations of proceedings are disfavored but may be granted upon the showing of good cause under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.341#stat.176.341.4">176.341, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>The compensation judge conducting the settlement conference may require the parties to present copies of all documentary evidence not previously filed and a summary of the evidence they will present at a formal hearing. If appropriate, a written summary decision shall be issued within ten days after the conference stating the issues and a determination of each issue. If a party fails to appear at the conference, all issues may be determined contrary to the absent party's interest, provided the party in attendance presents a prima facie case.</p>
          <p>The summary decision is final unless a written request for a formal hearing is served on all parties and filed with the commissioner within 30 days after the date of service and filing of the summary decision. Within ten days after receipt of the request, the commissioner shall certify the matter to the office for a de novo hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.305.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.13.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 13</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.305.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Testimony.</span></h2>
          <p>Where the chief administrative law judge has substituted a compensation judge originally assigned to hear a matter, the testimony taken before the substitute compensation judge shall be considered as though taken before the judge before whom it was originally assigned.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.305.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Striking from calendar.</span></h2>
          <p>A compensation judge, after receiving a properly served motion, may strike a case from the active trial calendar after the employee has been given 30 days to correct a deficient petition if it is shown that the information on the petition or included with the petition is incomplete. Once a case is stricken, it may not be reinstated until the missing information is provided to the adverse parties and filed with the compensation judge. If a case has been stricken from the calendar for 180 days or more and no corrective action has been taken, the compensation judge may, upon the judge's own motion or a motion of a party which is properly served on all parties, dismiss the case. The petitioner must be given at least 30 days' advance notice of the proposed dismissal before the dismissal is effective.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.305.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.47.0">1953 c 755 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/9/#laws.0.45.0">1969 c 9 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.93.0">1973 c 388 s 93</a>-95; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.106.0">1981 c 346 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.71.0">1987 c 332 s 71</a>-74; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/231/#laws.2.98.0">1995 c 231 art 2 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/294/#laws.0.4.0">1998 c 294 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/89/#laws.0.16.0">2011 c 89 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/182/#laws.0.7.0">2014 c 182 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.5.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/51/#laws.2.9.0">2023 c 51 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.42.0">2024 c 97 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.306">
        <h1 class="shn">176.306 SCHEDULED HEARINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.306.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Chief administrative law judge.</span></h2>
          <p>The chief administrative law judge shall schedule workers' compensation hearings on as regular a schedule as may be practicable in no fewer than six widely separated locations throughout the state, including at least four locations outside of the seven-county metropolitan area and Duluth, for the purpose of providing a convenient forum for parties to a compensation hearing and shall maintain a permanent office in Duluth staffed by at least one compensation judge. Continuances of the scheduled hearing date may be granted only under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.341#stat.176.341.4">176.341, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.306.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">District administrators; clerks of court.</span></h2>
          <p>The judicial district administrators or the court administrators of the county or district courts nearest to the locations selected by the chief administrative law judge pursuant to subdivision 1 shall provide suitable hearing rooms at the times and places agreed upon for the purpose of conducting workers' compensation hearings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.306.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Scheduling matters.</span></h2>
          <p>A compensation judge may schedule a pretrial or settlement conference, whether or not a party requests such a conference.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.306.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.107.0">1981 c 346 s 107</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.45.0">1982 c 424 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.75.0">1987 c 332 s 75</a>,76</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.307">
        <h1 class="shn">176.307 COMPENSATION JUDGES; BLOCK SYSTEM.</h1>
        <p>The chief administrative law judge may assign workers' compensation cases to compensation judges using a block system type of assignment that, among other things, ensures that a case will remain with the same judge from commencement to conclusion, except that the judge must be removed from the case when:</p>
        <p>(1) a party exercises a legal right to do so;</p>
        <p>(2) the judge is incapacitated or is otherwise unable to hold a hearing; or</p>
        <p>(3) assignment of a different judge is required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106#stat.176.106.7">176.106, subdivision 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.238#stat.176.238.6">176.238, subdivision 6</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.305#stat.176.305.1a">176.305, subdivision 1a</a>, or the Minnesota Code of Judicial Conduct.</p>
        <p>The block system shall be the preferred means of assigning cases, but it may be supplemented by other systems of case assignment to ensure that cases are timely decided.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.307.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/510/#laws.2.8.0">1992 c 510 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/89/#laws.0.18.0">2011 c 89 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/187/#laws.1.27.0">2012 c 187 art 1 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.31"><b>176.31</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.311">
        <h1 class="shn">176.311 REASSIGNMENT OF PETITION FOR HEARING.</h1>
        <p>Where a petition is heard before a compensation judge, at any time before an award or order has been made in such proceeding, the chief administrative law judge may reassign the petition for hearing before another compensation judge.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.311.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.48.0">1953 c 755 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.96.0">1973 c 388 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.108.0">1981 c 346 s 108</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.312">
        <h1 class="shn">176.312 AFFIDAVITS OF PREJUDICE AND PETITIONS FOR REASSIGNMENT.</h1>
        <p>In accordance with rules adopted by the chief administrative law judge, an affidavit of prejudice for cause may be filed by each party to the claim against a compensation judge assigned to hear a case.</p>
        <p>A petition for reassignment of a case to a different compensation judge for hearing may be filed once, in any case, by each party to the claim within 20 days after the filing party has received notice of the assigned judge. Upon receipt of a timely petition for reassignment, the chief administrative law judge shall assign the case to another judge.</p>
        <p>An affidavit of prejudice or a petition for reassignment shall be filed with the chief administrative law judge and shall not result in the continuance or delay of a hearing scheduled under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.341">176.341</a>.</p>
        <p>This section does not apply to prehearing, settlement conferences, or administrative conferences.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.312.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.144.0">1983 c 290 s 144</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.77.0">1987 c 332 s 77</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/7/#laws.12.15.0">1Sp2019 c 7 art 12 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.32"><b>176.32</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.321">
        <h1 class="shn">176.321 ANSWER TO PETITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.321.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Filing, service.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 30 days after service of the petition, an adverse party shall serve and file an answer to the petition. The party shall serve a copy of the answer on the petitioner or the petitioner's attorney.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.321.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>The answer shall admit, deny, or affirmatively defend against the substantial averments of the petition, and shall state the contention of the adverse party with reference to the matter in dispute.</p>
          <p>Each fact alleged by the petition or answer and not specifically denied by the answer or reply is deemed admitted, but the failure to deny such a fact does not preclude the compensation judge from requiring proof of the fact.</p>
          <p>The answer shall include the names and addresses of all known witnesses; whether or not the employer intends to schedule an adverse examination and, if known, the date, time, and place of all adverse examinations; the desired location for a hearing; any request for a prehearing or settlement conference; the estimated time needed to present evidence at a hearing; and, if an affidavit of significant financial hardship and request for an expedited hearing are included with the petition, any objection the employer may have to that request. If the date, time, and place of all adverse examinations is unknown at the time the answer is filed, the employer must notify the office in writing of the date, time, and place of all adverse examinations within 50 days of the filing of the claim petition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.321.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Extension of time in which to file answer.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon showing of cause, the office may extend the time in which to file an answer or reply for not more than 30 additional days. The time to file an answer or reply may also be extended upon agreement of the petitioner, and provided that the office must be notified in writing by the employer no later than five days beyond the time required for the filing of the answer of the fact that an agreement has been reached, including the length of the extension. Any case received by the office that does not include an answer, written extension order, or written notification of the extension agreement shall be immediately set for pretrial conference and hearing at the first available date under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.331">176.331</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.321.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.49.0">1953 c 755 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.97.0">1973 c 388 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.109.0">1981 c 346 s 109</a>,110; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.145.0">1983 c 290 s 145</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.78.0">1987 c 332 s 78</a>,79; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.7.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.43.0">2024 c 97 s 43</a>,44</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.322">
        <h1 class="shn">176.322 DECISIONS BASED ON STIPULATED FACTS.</h1>
        <p>If the parties agree to a stipulated set of facts and only legal issues remain, the compensation judge may determine the matter without a hearing based upon the stipulated facts and the determination is appealable to the court of appeals pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.421">176.421</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.442">176.442</a>. In any case where a stipulated set of facts has been submitted pursuant to this section, upon receipt of the file or the stipulated set of facts the chief administrative law judge shall immediately assign the case to a compensation judge for a determination. The judge shall issue a determination within 60 days after receipt of the stipulated facts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.322.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.80.0">1987 c 332 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.45.0">2024 c 97 s 45</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.325">
        <h1 class="shn">176.325 CERTIFIED QUESTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.325.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When certified.</span></h2>
          <p>The chief administrative law judge or commissioner may certify a question of workers' compensation law to the supreme court as important and doubtful under the following circumstances:</p>
          <p>(1) all parties to the case have stipulated in writing to the facts; and</p>
          <p>(2) the issue to be resolved is a question of workers' compensation law that has not been resolved by the Minnesota Supreme Court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.325.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Expedited decision.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the legislature's intent that the Minnesota Supreme Court resolve the certified question as expeditiously as possible, after compliance by the parties with any requirements of the Minnesota Supreme Court regarding submission of legal memoranda, oral argument, or other matters, and after the participation of amicus curiae, should the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals or Minnesota Supreme Court consider such participation advisable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.325.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner or chief administrative law judge shall notify all persons who request to be notified of a certification under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.325.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/510/#laws.2.9.0">1992 c 510 art 2 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.33"><b>176.33</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.331">
        <h1 class="shn">176.331 PROCEEDINGS WHEN ANSWER NOT FILED.</h1>
        <p class="in">Except in cases involving multiple employers or multiple insurers, if an adverse party fails to file and serve an answer or obtain an extension from the office or the petitioner as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.321#stat.176.321.3">176.321, subdivision 3</a>, the office shall set the matter for an immediate pretrial conference and hearing for prompt award or other order. The adverse party that failed to file an answer or appear at a pretrial conference may appear at the hearing, present evidence and question witnesses, but shall not be granted a continuance except upon a showing of good cause.</p>
        <p class="in">If an adverse party who fails to serve and file an answer is neither insured for workers' compensation liability nor a licensed self-insured as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.181">176.181 </a>and the special compensation fund is a party to the proceeding, the compensation judge may enter an order awarding benefits to the petitioning party without a hearing if so requested by the special compensation fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.331.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.50.0">1953 c 755 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.98.0">1973 c 388 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.111.0">1981 c 346 s 111</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.146.0">1983 c 290 s 146</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.81.0">1987 c 332 s 81</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/110/#laws.3.5.0">2016 c 110 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.9.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/51/#laws.2.10.0">2023 c 51 art 2 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.34"><b>176.34</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.341">
        <h1 class="shn">176.341 HEARING ON PETITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.341.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Time.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the filing of a petition, the chief administrative law judge shall fix a time and place for hearing the petition.
