The hearing shall be held as soon as practicable and at a time and place determined by the chief administrative law judge to be the most convenient for the parties, keeping in mind the intent of chapter 176 and the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.306">176.306</a>. Except where a shorter time period is required under this chapter, all hearings must be held within 26 months after a petition has been filed, unless the chief administrative law judge issues an order for a later date for the hearing explaining why the hearing could not be held within 26 months.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.341.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Place.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise ordered by the chief administrative law judge, the hearing shall be held in the county where the injury or death occurred.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.341.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice mailed to each party.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless subdivision 6 applies, at least 30 days prior to the date of hearing, the chief administrative law judge shall mail a notice of the time and place of hearing to each interested party. This subdivision does not apply to hearings which have been continued from an earlier date. In those cases, the notice shall be given in a manner deemed appropriate by the chief administrative law judge after considering the particular circumstances in each case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.341.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Continuances.</span></h2>
          <p>Only the chief administrative law judge or designee, on a showing of good cause, may grant a continuance of a hearing at the office. Except in cases of emergency or other good cause shown, any request for a continuance must be signed by both the party and the attorney seeking the continuance.</p>
          <p>A continuance of a hearing will be granted only upon a showing of good cause. Good cause is established when the underlying eventuality is unforeseen, is not due to lack of preparation, is relevant, is brought to the chief administrative law judge's attention in a timely manner and does not prejudice the adversary.</p>
          <p>Continuances will not be granted for the reason that an attorney for one of the parties has scheduled a vacation for the date set for the hearing unless the attorney has, prior to the setting of the hearing date, notified the office of the unavailable dates.</p>
          <p>Continuances which are requested during the course of a hearing are subject to the same standards but may be granted or denied by the compensation judge assigned to the hearing. Continuances of prehearing or settlement conferences at the office are subject to the same standards but may be granted or denied by a compensation judge, the calendar judge, or other presiding officer assigned to the prehearing or settlement conference. Cancellation of settlement conferences shall be granted if all parties agree to cancellation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.341.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Evidence.</span></h2>
          <p>Absent a clear showing of surprise at the hearing or the unexpected unavailability of a crucial witness, all evidence must be submitted at the time of the hearing. Upon a showing of good cause, the compensation judge may grant an extension not to exceed 30 days following the hearing date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.341.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Significant financial hardship; expedited hearings.</span></h2>
          <p>An employee may file a request for an expedited hearing which must be granted upon a showing of significant financial hardship. In determining whether a significant financial hardship exists, consideration shall be given to whether the employee is presently employed, the employee's income from all sources, the nature and extent of the employee's expenses and debts, whether the employee is the sole support of any dependents, whether either foreclosure of homestead property or repossession of necessary personal property is imminent, and any other matters which have a direct bearing on the employee's ability to provide food, clothing, and shelter for the employee and any dependents.</p>
          <p>A request for an expedited hearing must be accompanied by a sworn affidavit of the employee providing facts necessary to satisfy the criteria for a significant financial hardship. The request may be made at the time a claim petition is filed or any time thereafter. Unless the employer objects to the request in the answer to the claim petition or within 30 calendar days of the filing of a request made subsequent to the filing of the claim petition, the affidavit is a sufficient showing of significant financial hardship.</p>
          <p>If a request for an expedited hearing has been served and filed, the compensation judge shall grant or deny the request, provided that where the parties agree that significant financial hardship exists or no objection to the request is timely filed, the request is automatically granted. If it is denied, the matter will be returned to the regular calendar of cases and the request for an expedited hearing may be renewed at a settlement conference.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.341.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.51.0">1953 c 755 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.99.0">1973 c 388 s 99</a>-101; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.112.0">1981 c 346 s 112</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.147.0">1983 c 290 s 147</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.82.0">1987 c 332 s 82</a>-85; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/366/#laws.0.89.0">1998 c 366 s 89</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/75/#laws.0.15.0">2009 c 75 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/89/#laws.0.19.0">2011 c 89 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.10.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.46.0">2024 c 97 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.35"><b>176.35</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.351">
        <h1 class="shn">176.351 TESTIMONIAL POWERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.351.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Oaths.</span></h2>
          <p>The compensation judge to whom a petition has been assigned for hearing shall administer an oath to each witness. The commissioner may also administer an oath when required in the performance of duties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.351.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Subpoenas.</span></h2>
          <p>Acting with or without the written request of an interested party, the commissioner or compensation judge before whom a hearing is held may issue a subpoena for the attendance of a witness or the production of such books, papers, records and documents as are material in the cause and are designated in the subpoena. The commissioner may also issue a subpoena for the attendance of a witness or the production of such books, papers, records, and documents as are material in the cause pending and are designated in the subpoena.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.351.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Subpoenas not permitted of decision makers.</span></h2>
          <p>A member of the rehabilitation review panel or Medical Services Board or an employee of the department who has conducted an administrative or settlement conference or hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106">176.106</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.239">176.239</a>, or who has certified or has declined to certify a dispute under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.081#stat.176.081.1">176.081, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), shall not be subpoenaed to testify regarding the conference, hearing, dispute certification, or concerning a mediation session. A member of the rehabilitation review panel, Medical Services Board, or an employee of the department may be required to answer written interrogatories limited to the following questions:</p>
          <p>(a) Were all statutory and administrative procedural rules adhered to in reaching the decision?</p>
          <p>(b) If the answer to question (a) is no, what deviations took place?</p>
          <p>(c) Did the person making the decision consider all the information presented prior to rendering a decision?</p>
          <p>(d) Did the person making the decision rely on information outside of the information presented at the conference or hearing in making the decision?</p>
          <p>(e) If the answer to question (d) is yes, what other information was relied upon in making the decision?</p>
          <p>In addition, for a hearing with a compensation judge and with the consent of the compensation judge, an employee of the department who conducted an administrative conference, hearing, or mediation session, may be requested to answer written interrogatories relating to statements made by a party at the prior proceeding. These interrogatories shall be limited to affirming or denying that specific statements were made by a party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.351.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Subpoenas not permitted of department employees who provide assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and any employee of the department shall not be subject to a subpoena for purposes of providing expert testimony or describing the nature of assistance or advice provided under this chapter. This prohibition does not apply to: testimony of a department employee in a workers' compensation enforcement proceeding brought by the commissioner; a dispute in which the commissioner or the special compensation fund is a party; or a qualified rehabilitation consultant, qualified rehabilitation consultant intern, or job placement coordinator employed in the department's vocational rehabilitation unit established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.104">176.104</a>, who has provided rehabilitation, job placement, or job development services under a rehabilitation plan for an employee with a workers' compensation claim.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.351.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Advancement of fees and costs.</span></h2>
          <p>The person who applies for issuance of a subpoena shall advance the required service and witness fees. The commissioner shall pay for the attendance of witnesses who are subpoenaed by the commissioner. The chief administrative law judge shall pay for the attendance of witnesses who are subpoenaed by a compensation judge. The fees are the same as the service and witness fees in civil actions in district court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.351.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Proceedings as for contempt of court.</span></h2>
          <p>Where a person does not comply with an order or subpoena, the commissioner or compensation judge concerned, may apply to the district court in the county in which the petition is pending for issuance of an order compelling obedience. Upon such an application, the district court shall compel obedience to the order or subpoena by attachment proceedings as for contempt in the case of disobedience of a similar order or subpoena issued by the district court.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.351.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.52.0">1953 c 755 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.102.0">1973 c 388 s 102</a>-105; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.113.0">1981 c 346 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/432/#laws.2.44.0">1984 c 432 art 2 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.86.0">1987 c 332 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/75/#laws.0.16.0">2009 c 75 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/12/#laws.0.11.0">2021 c 12 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.36"><b>176.36</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.361">
        <h1 class="shn">176.361 INTERVENTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Right to intervene.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A person who has an interest in any matter before the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, or commissioner, or office such that the person may either gain or lose by an order or decision may intervene in the proceeding by filing a motion in writing stating the facts which show the interest. The commissioner is considered to have an interest and shall be permitted to intervene at the appellate level when a party relies in its claim or defense upon any statute or rule administered by the commissioner, or upon any rule, order, requirement, or agreement issued or made under the statute or rule.</p>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may adopt rules, not inconsistent with this section to govern intervention. The Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals shall adopt rules to govern the procedure for intervention in matters before it. The office may adopt rules to govern the procedure for intervention in matters before it.</p>
          <p class="in">If the Department of Human Services or the Department of Employment and Economic Development seeks to intervene in any matter before the division, a compensation judge or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, a nonattorney employee of the department, acting at the direction of the staff of the attorney general, may prepare, sign, serve and file motions for intervention and related documents, attend prehearing conferences, and participate in matters before a compensation judge or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals. Any other interested party may intervene using a nonattorney and may participate in any proceeding to the same extent an attorney could. This activity shall not be considered to be the unauthorized practice of law. An intervenor represented by a nonattorney shall be deemed to be represented by an attorney for the purposes of the conclusive presumption of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.521#stat.176.521.2">176.521, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">Subdivisions 3 to 6 do not apply to the following proceedings conducted by the Department of Labor and Industry or the office: mediation proceedings; discontinuance conferences under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.239">176.239</a>; or administrative conferences under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106">176.106</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Written motion.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A person desiring to intervene in a workers' compensation case as a party, including but not limited to a health care provider who has rendered services to an employee or an insurer who has paid benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.191">176.191</a>, shall submit a timely written motion to intervene to the commissioner, the office, or to the court of appeals, whichever is applicable.</p>
          <p class="in">(a) The motion must be served on all parties, except for other intervenors, either personally, by first class mail, or by registered mail, return receipt requested. A motion to intervene must be served and filed within 60 days after a potential intervenor has been served with notice of a right to intervene or within 30 days of notice of an administrative conference or expedited hearing. Upon the filing of a timely motion to intervene, the potential intervenor shall be granted intervenor status without the need for an order. Objections to the intervention may be subsequently addressed by a compensation judge. Where a motion to intervene is not timely filed under this section, the potential intervenor interest shall be extinguished and the potential intervenor may not collect, or attempt to collect, the extinguished interest from the employee, employer, insurer, or any government program.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The motion must show how the applicant's legal rights, duties, or privileges may be determined or affected by the case; state the grounds and purposes for which intervention is sought; and indicate the statutory right to intervene. The motion must be accompanied by the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) an itemization of disability payments showing the period during which the payments were or are being made; the weekly or monthly rate of the payments; and the amount of reimbursement claimed;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a summary of the medical or treatment payments, or rehabilitation services provided by the Vocational Rehabilitation Unit, broken down by creditor, showing the total bill submitted, the period of treatment or rehabilitation covered by that bill, the amount of payment on that bill, and to whom the payment was made;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) copies of all medical or treatment bills for which payment is sought;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) copies of the work sheets or other information stating how the payments on medical or treatment bills were calculated;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) a copy of the relevant policy or contract provisions upon which the claim for reimbursement is based;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) the name and telephone number of the person representing the intervenor who has authority to represent the intervenor, including but not limited to the authority to reach a settlement of the issues in dispute;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) proof of service or copy of the registered mail receipt evidencing service on all parties except for other intervenors;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) at the option of the intervenor, a proposed stipulation which states that all of the payments for which reimbursement is claimed are related to the injury or condition in dispute in the case and that, if the petitioner is successful in proving the compensability of the claim, it is agreed that the sum be reimbursed to the intervenor; and</p>
          <p class="in">(9) if represented by an attorney, the name, address, telephone number, and Minnesota Supreme Court license number of the attorney.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Stipulation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">If the person filing a timely motion to intervene has included a proposed stipulation, all parties shall either execute and return the signed stipulation to the intervenor who must file it with the division or judge or serve upon the intervenor and all other parties and file with the division specific and detailed objections to any services rendered or payments made by the intervenor which are not conceded to be correct and related to the injury or condition the petitioner has asserted is compensable. If a party has not returned the signed stipulation or filed specific and detailed objections within 30 days of service of the motion to intervene, the intervenor's right to reimbursement for the amount sought is deemed established provided that the petitioner's claim is determined to be compensable. The office may establish procedures for filing objections if a timely motion to intervene is filed less than 30 days before a scheduled hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Attendance by intervenor.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A person who has submitted a timely written motion to intervene, as required by subdivision 2, is not required to attend settlement or pretrial conferences or the hearing, unless attendance is ordered by the compensation judge assigned to the case, pursuant to a motion to require the intervenor's attendance filed by a party or as a matter of the judge's discretion. A motion to require attendance must be served and filed at least 20 days before a scheduled proceeding, and the compensation judge must serve and file an order granting or denying the motion at least ten days before a scheduled proceeding. If attendance is ordered, failure of the intervenor to attend a proceeding either in person or, if approved by the compensation judge, by telephone or some other electronic medium, shall result in the denial of the claim for reimbursement except upon a showing of good cause. If attendance has not been ordered, this subdivision does not prohibit an intervenor from attending a conference or hearing in person, or from requesting permission from the compensation judge to attend a conference or hearing by telephone or other electronic medium.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Objections.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">If a specific and detailed objection to intervention remains following settlement or pretrial conferences, the issue shall be addressed at the hearing. If the intervenor has not been ordered to attend the hearing pursuant to subdivision 4, or has received permission to attend the hearing by telephone or other electronic medium, the intervenor may provide a written response to the objection before the hearing according to subdivision 6 for consideration as a matter of discretion by the judge.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Presentation of evidence by intervenor.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Unless a stipulation has been signed and filed or the intervenor's right to reimbursement has otherwise been established, the intervenor shall present evidence in support of the claim at or before the hearing. When the intervenor has not been ordered to attend the hearing pursuant to subdivision 4, or has received permission to attend the hearing by telephone or other electronic medium, the office may establish a procedure for submission of the intervenor's evidence and response to outstanding objections to intervention. If the intervenor does not submit a written response to the objection before the hearing, the compensation judge's determination on the objection must be based on the information and evidence submitted prior to or at the hearing, as a matter of judicial discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Effects of noncompliance.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivisions 2 and 4, failure to comply with this section shall not result in a denial of the claim for reimbursement unless the compensation judge, or commissioner, determines that the noncompliance has materially prejudiced the interests of the other parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.361.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Chief administrative law judge orders.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The chief administrative law judge may issue standing orders to implement this section. The chief administrative law judge has the authority to issue standing orders instead of, or in addition to, the authority granted to the office or compensation judges under this section, provided that any standing order issued by the chief administrative law judge must be consistent with this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.361.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.53.0">1953 c 755 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.106.0">1973 c 388 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.148.0">1983 c 290 s 148</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/432/#laws.2.45.0">1984 c 432 art 2 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/461/#laws.0.30.0">1986 c 461 s 30</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.87.0">1987 c 332 s 87</a>-89; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/464/#laws.1.5.0">1992 c 464 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/262/#laws.0.21.0">2002 c 262 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/110/#laws.3.6.0">2016 c 110 art 3 s 6</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.5.1.0">2017 c 94 art 5 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.47.0">2024 c 97 s 47</a>,48</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.37"><b>176.37</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.371">
        <h1 class="shn">176.371 AWARD OR DISALLOWANCE OF COMPENSATION.</h1>
        <p>The compensation judge to whom a petition has been assigned for hearing, shall hear all competent, relevant evidence produced at the hearing. All questions of fact and law submitted to a compensation judge at the hearing shall be disposed of and the judge's decision shall be filed with the commissioner, except where expedited procedures require a shorter time, within 60 days after the submission, unless sickness or casualty prevents a timely filing, or the chief administrative law judge extends the time for good cause. The compensation judge's decision shall include a determination of all contested issues of fact and law and an award or disallowance of compensation or other order as the pleadings, evidence, this chapter and rule require. A compensation judge's decision shall include a memorandum only if necessary to delineate the reasons for the decision or to discuss the credibility of witnesses. A memorandum shall not contain a recitation of the evidence presented at the hearing but shall be limited to the compensation judge's basis for the decision.</p>
        <p>No part of the salary of a compensation judge shall be paid unless the chief administrative law judge determines that all decisions of that judge have been issued within the time limits prescribed by this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.371.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.54.0">1953 c 755 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.114.0">1981 c 346 s 114</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.149.0">1983 c 290 s 149</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.90.0">1987 c 332 s 90</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.38"><b>176.38</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.381">
        <h1 class="shn">176.381 REFERENCE OF QUESTIONS OF FACT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.381.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hearing before Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals.</span></h2>
          <p>In the hearing of any matter before the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, the chief judge of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals may refer any question of fact to the chief administrative law judge for assignment to a compensation judge either to hear evidence and report it to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals or to hear evidence and make findings of fact and report them to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals. The Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals shall notify the commissioner of any matter referred to a compensation judge under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.381.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Hearing before compensation judge.</span></h2>
          <p>In the hearing of any petition before a compensation judge, the chief administrative law judge may refer any question of fact to another compensation judge to hear evidence and report it to the original compensation judge.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.381.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.55.0">1953 c 755 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.107.0">1973 c 388 s 107</a>,108; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.115.0">1981 c 346 s 115</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.39"><b>176.39</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.391">
        <h1 class="shn">176.391 INVESTIGATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.391.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Power to make.</span></h2>
          <p>Before, during, or after any hearing, a compensation judge may make an independent investigation of the facts alleged in the petition or answer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.391.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appointment of physicians, surgeons, and other experts.</span></h2>
          <p>The compensation judge assigned to a matter may appoint one or more neutral physicians or surgeons to examine the injury of the employee and report thereon except as provided otherwise pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.1361">176.1361</a>. Where necessary to determine the facts, the services of other experts may also be employed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.391.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The report of a physician, surgeon, or other expert requested under this section shall be filed with the compensation judge assigned to the matter if any. The report shall be made a part of the record of the case and be open to inspection as such.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.391.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>The compensation judge shall fix the compensation of a physician, surgeon, or other expert whose services are employed under this section. This compensation shall be paid initially out of the funds appropriated for the maintenance of the Workers' Compensation Division, but shall be taxed as costs to either party, or both, or otherwise, as the compensation judge directs.</p>
          <p>Where a sum which has been taxed to a party has not been paid, it may be collected in the same manner as are costs generally.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.391.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.56.0">1953 c 755 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/9/#laws.0.46.0">1969 c 9 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.109.0">1973 c 388 s 109</a>-112; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/1/3/#laws.0.59.0">Ex1979 c 3 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.116.0">1981 c 346 s 116</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/90/#laws.0.19.0">2005 c 90 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.11.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.40"><b>176.40</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.401">
        <h1 class="shn">176.401 HEARINGS PUBLIC.</h1>
        <p>All hearings before a compensation judge are public.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.57.0">1953 c 755 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.117.0">1981 c 346 s 117</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.41"><b>176.41</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.411">
        <h1 class="shn">176.411 RULES OF EVIDENCE, PLEADING, AND PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Conduct of hearings and investigations.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided by this chapter, when a compensation judge makes an investigation or conducts a hearing, the compensation judge is bound neither by the common law or statutory rules of evidence nor by technical or formal rules of pleading or procedure. Hearsay evidence which is reliable is admissible. The investigation or hearing shall be conducted in a manner to ascertain the substantial rights of the parties.</p>
          <p>Findings of fact shall be based upon relevant and material evidence only, as presented by competent witnesses, and shall comport with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.021">176.021</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Depositions.</span></h2>
          <p>Except where a compensation judge orders otherwise, depositions may be taken in the manner which the law provides for depositions in civil actions in district court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.411.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Hospital records as evidence.</span></h2>
          <p>A hospital record relating to medical or surgical treatment given an employee is admissible as evidence of the medical and surgical matters stated in the record, but it is not conclusive proof of such matters.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.58.0">1953 c 755 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.118.0">1981 c 346 s 118</a>,119; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.91.0">1987 c 332 s 91</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.42"><b>176.42</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">APPEALS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.421">
        <h1 class="shn">176.421 APPEALS TO WORKERS' COMPENSATION COURT OF APPEALS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Time for taking; grounds.</span></h2>
          <p>When a petition has been heard before a compensation judge, within 30 days after a party in interest has been served with notice of an award or disallowance of compensation, or other order affecting the merits of the case, the party may appeal to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals on any of the following grounds:</p>
          <p>(1) the order does not conform with this chapter; or</p>
          <p>(2) the compensation judge committed an error of law; or</p>
          <p>(3) the findings of fact and order were clearly erroneous and unsupported by substantial evidence in view of the entire record as submitted; or</p>
          <p>(4) the findings of fact and order were procured by fraud, or coercion, or other improper conduct of a party in interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Extension of time.</span></h2>
          <p>Where a party shows cause within the 30-day period referred to in subdivision 1, the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals may extend the time for taking the appeal for not more than 30 additional days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice of appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>The appellant or the appellant's attorney shall prepare and sign a written notice of appeal specifying:</p>
          <p>(1) the order appealed from;</p>
          <p>(2) that appellant appeals from the order to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals;</p>
          <p>(3) the particular finding of fact or conclusion of law which the appellant claims was unsupported by substantial evidence in view of the entire record as submitted or procured by fraud, coercion, or other improper conduct; and</p>
          <p>(4) any other ground upon which the appeal is taken.</p>
          <p>An appeal initiates the preparation of a typewritten transcript of the entire record unless the appeal is solely from an award of attorney's fees or an award of costs and disbursements or unless otherwise ordered by the court of appeals. On appeals from an award of attorney's fees or an award of costs and disbursements, the appellant must specifically delineate in the notice of appeal the portions of the record to be transcribed in order for the court of appeals to consider the appeal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Cross-appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>The respondent may cross-appeal within the 30-day period for taking an appeal, or within 15 days after service of the notice of appeal on that respondent, whichever is later.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Service and filing of notice; cost of transcript.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the 30-day period for taking an appeal, the appellant shall:</p>
          <p>(1) serve a copy of the notice of appeal on each adverse party; and</p>
          <p>(2) pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.285">176.285</a>, file the original notice of appeal, with proof of service by admission or affidavit, with the chief administrative law judge and file a copy with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>In order to defray the cost of the preparation of the record of the proceedings appealed from, each appellant and cross-appellant shall pay to the commissioner of management and budget, Office of Administrative Hearings account the sum of $25. The filing fee must be received by the Office of Administrative Hearings within ten business days after the end of the appeal period. If the filing fee is not received within ten days after the appeal period, the appeal is not timely filed.</p>
          <p>The first party to file an appeal is liable for the original cost of preparation of the transcript. Cross-appellants or any other persons requesting a copy of the transcript are liable for the cost of the copy. The chief administrative law judge may require payment for transcription costs to be made in advance of the transcript preparation. The cost of a transcript prepared by a nongovernmental source shall be paid directly to that source and shall not exceed the cost that the source would be able to charge the state for the same service.</p>
          <p>Upon a showing of cause, the chief administrative law judge may direct that a transcript be prepared without expense to the party requesting its preparation, in which case the cost of the transcript shall be paid by the Office of Administrative Hearings.</p>
          <p>All fees received by the Office of Administrative Hearings for the preparation of the record for submission to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals or for the cost of transcripts prepared by the office shall be deposited in the Office of Administrative Hearings account in the state treasury and shall be used solely for the purpose of keeping the record of hearings conducted under this chapter and the preparation of transcripts of those hearings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Transcript; certification of the record.</span></h2>
          <p>When the notice of appeal has been filed with the chief administrative law judge and the fee for the preparation of the record has been paid, the chief administrative law judge shall immediately order the preparation of a typewritten transcript of that part of the hearing delineated in the notice. The official reporter or other person designated by the chief administrative law judge who transcribes the proceedings shall certify to their correctness.</p>
          <p>If the transcript is prepared by a person who is not an employee of the Office of Administrative Hearings, upon completion of the transcript, the original shall be filed with the chief administrative law judge.</p>
          <p>When the transcript has been completed and is on file with the chief administrative law judge, the chief judge shall certify the record to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals and notify the commissioner of the certification.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Powers of Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals on appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>On an appeal taken under this section, the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals' review is limited to the issues raised by the parties in the notice of appeal or by a cross-appeal. In these cases, on those issues raised by the appeal, the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals may:</p>
          <p>(1) grant an oral argument based on the record before the compensation judge;</p>
          <p>(2) examine the record;</p>
          <p>(3) substitute for the findings of fact made by the compensation judge findings based on the total evidence;</p>
          <p>(4) sustain, reverse, make or modify an award or disallowance of compensation or other order based on the facts, findings, and law; and</p>
          <p>(5) remand or make other appropriate order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Time limit for decision.</span></h2>
          <p>The court shall issue a decision in each case within 90 days after certification of the record to the court by the chief administrative law judge, the filing of a cross-appeal, oral argument, or a final submission of briefs or memoranda by the parties, whichever is latest. For cases submitted without oral argument, a decision shall be issued within 90 days after assignment of the case to the judges. The chief judge may waive the 90-day limitation for any proceeding before the court for good cause shown. No part of the salary of a Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals judge may be paid unless the judge, upon accepting the payment, certifies that decisions in cases in which the judge has participated have been issued within the time limits prescribed by this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.421.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Record of proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>At the office's own expense, the office shall make a complete record of all formal proceedings before the office.</p>
          <p>The office shall furnish a transcript of these proceedings to any person who requests it and who pays a reasonable charge which shall be set by the office. Upon a showing of cause, the chief administrative law judge may direct that a transcript be prepared without expense to the person requesting the transcript, in which case the cost of the transcript shall be paid by the office. Transcript fees received under this subdivision shall be paid to the Workers' Compensation Division account in the state treasury and shall be annually appropriated to the division for the sole purpose of providing a record and transcripts as provided in this subdivision. This subdivision does not apply to any administrative conference or other proceeding before the commissioner which may be heard de novo in another proceeding including but not limited to proceedings under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106">176.106</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.239">176.239</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.421.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.59.0">1953 c 755 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.113.0">1973 c 388 s 113</a>-115; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.120.0">1981 c 346 s 120</a>-124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/3/2/#laws.1.21.0">3Sp1981 c 2 art 1 s 21</a>-23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.150.0">1983 c 290 s 150</a>-153; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.148.0">1983 c 301 s 148</a>-150; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/432/#laws.2.46.0">1984 c 432 art 2 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/461/#laws.0.32.0">1986 c 461 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.92.0">1987 c 332 s 92</a>,93; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.2.25.0">1989 c 209 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/426/#laws.1.23.0">1990 c 426 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/571/#laws.0.38.0">1990 c 571 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.79.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 79</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/510/#laws.2.10.0">1992 c 510 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/178/#laws.0.2.0">2006 c 178 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/32/#laws.2.12.0">2022 c 32 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/97/#laws.0.49.0">2024 c 97 s 49</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.43"><b>176.43</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.431"><b>176.431</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/461/#laws.0.37.0">1986 c 461 s 37</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.44"><b>176.44</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.176.441">
        <h1>176.441 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.441.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/461/#laws.0.37.0">1986 c 461 s 37</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.441.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.145.0">1981 c 346 s 145</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/461/#laws.0.37.0">1986 c 461 s 37</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.442">
        <h1 class="shn">176.442 APPEALS FROM DECISIONS OF COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <p>Except for a commissioner's decision which may be heard de novo in another proceeding including but not limited to a decision from an administrative conference under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.102">176.102</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.103">176.103</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.106">176.106</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.239">176.239</a>, or a summary decision under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.305">176.305</a>, any decision or determination of the commissioner affecting a right, privilege, benefit, or duty which is imposed or conferred under this chapter is subject to review by the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals. A person aggrieved by the determination may appeal to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals by filing a notice of appeal with the commissioner in the same manner and within the same time as if the appeal were from an order or decision of a compensation judge to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.442.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.119.0">1973 c 388 s 119</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.154.0">1983 c 290 s 154</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/432/#laws.2.47.0">1984 c 432 art 2 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.94.0">1987 c 332 s 94</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.3.3.0">1987 c 384 art 3 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.445"><b>176.445</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/123/#laws.0.23.0">2001 c 123 s 23</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.45"><b>176.45</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.451">
        <h1 class="shn">176.451 DEFAULTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.451.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application to district court for judgment.</span></h2>
          <p>Where there has been a default of more than 30 days in the payment of compensation due under an award, the employee, or the employee's dependent, or other person entitled to the payment of money under the award, may apply to the judge of any district court for the entry of judgment upon the award.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.451.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Certified copy of award; filing, notice.</span></h2>
          <p>The application shall be made by filing a certified copy of the award with the court administrator and by serving a ten days' notice upon adverse parties. Service of the notice shall be made in the manner provided by court rule for service of summons in district court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.451.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Court administrator's fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The court administrator shall charge $5 for the entire service the court administrator performs under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.451.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Matters for determination; judgment.</span></h2>
          <p>When a judge hears the application for judgment upon the award, the judge has authority to determine only the facts of the award and the regularity of the proceedings upon which the award is based. The judge shall enter judgment accordingly.</p>
          <p>Judgment shall not be entered upon an award while an appeal is pending.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.451.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effect of district court judgment.</span></h2>
          <p>The judgment of the district court entered upon an award has the same force and effect, and may be vacated, set aside, or satisfied as may other judgments of the district court.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.451.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.62.0">1953 c 755 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/442/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 442 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.46"><b>176.46</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.461">
        <h1 class="shn">176.461 SETTING ASIDE AWARD.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except when a writ of certiorari has been issued by the supreme court and the matter is still pending in that court or if as a matter of law the determination of the supreme court cannot be subsequently modified, the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, for cause, at any time after an award, upon application of either party and not less than five working days after written notice to all interested parties, may set the award aside and grant a new hearing and refer the matter for a determination on its merits to the chief administrative law judge for assignment to a compensation judge, who shall make findings of fact, conclusions of law, and an order of award or disallowance of compensation or other order based on the pleadings and the evidence produced and as required by the provisions of this chapter or rules adopted under it.</p>
        <p>(b) As used in this section, the phrase "for cause" is limited to the following:</p>
        <p>(1) a mutual mistake of fact;</p>
        <p>(2) newly discovered evidence;</p>
        <p>(3) fraud; or</p>
        <p>(4) a substantial change in medical condition since the time of the award that was clearly not anticipated and could not reasonably have been anticipated at the time of the award.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.461.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.63.0">1953 c 755 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/40/#laws.0.15.0">Ex1967 c 40 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.120.0">1973 c 388 s 120</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.18.0">1975 c 359 s 18</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.127.0">1981 c 346 s 127</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/290/#laws.0.155.0">1983 c 290 s 155</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/510/#laws.2.11.0">1992 c 510 art 2 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.47"><b>176.47</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.471">
        <h1 class="shn">176.471 REVIEW BY SUPREME COURT ON CERTIORARI.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Time for seeking review; grounds.</span></h2>
          <p>Where the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals has made an award or disallowance of compensation or other order, a party in interest who acts within 30 days from the date the party was served with notice of the order may have the order reviewed by the supreme court on certiorari upon one of the following grounds:</p>
          <p>(1) the order does not conform with this chapter;</p>
          <p>(2) the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals committed any other error of law; or</p>
          <p>(3) the findings of fact and order were unsupported by substantial evidence in view of the entire record as submitted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Extension of time for seeking review or for filing other papers.</span></h2>
          <p>Where cause is shown within the 30-day period referred to in subdivision 1, the supreme court may extend the time for seeking review on certiorari. The supreme court may also extend the time for filing any other paper which this chapter requires to be filed with that court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Service of writ; filing fee.</span></h2>
          <p>To effect a review upon certiorari, the party shall serve a writ of certiorari upon the administrator of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals within the 30-day period referred to in subdivision 1. The party shall also at this time pay to the clerk of the appellate courts the fee prescribed by <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/ap/subtype/rcap/id/116/#116.03">rule 116.03</a> of the Rules of Civil Appellate Procedure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Contents of writ.</span></h2>
          <p>The writ of certiorari required by subdivision 3 shall show that a review is to be had in the supreme court of the proceedings of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals upon which the order is based.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Bond.</span></h2>
          <p>The Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals may, upon motion of any respondent and a showing that extraordinary circumstances warrant the requirement of a cost bond, order that a bond be provided as prescribed by <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/ap/subtype/rcap/id/107/">rule 107.02</a> of the Rules of Civil Appellate Procedure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Transmittal of fee and return.</span></h2>
          <p>When the writ of certiorari has been served upon the administrator of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, the bond has been filed, and the filing fee has been paid, the administrator shall immediately transmit to the clerk of the appellate courts that filing fee and the return to the writ of certiorari and bond.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Jurisdiction vested.</span></h2>
          <p>Filing such return and payment of the filing fee referred to in subdivision 6 vests the supreme court with jurisdiction of the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Return of proceedings transmitted to court.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 30 days after the writ of certiorari, bond, and filing fee have been filed with the administrator of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, the administrator shall transmit to the clerk of the appellate courts a true and complete return of the proceedings of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals under review, or the part of those proceedings necessary to allow the supreme court to review properly the questions presented.</p>
          <p>The Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals shall certify the return of the proceedings under its seal. The petitioner or relator shall pay to the administrator of the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals the reasonable expense of preparing the return.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Application of rules governing appeals in civil actions.</span></h2>
          <p>When the return of the proceedings before the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals has been filed with the clerk of the appellate courts, the supreme court shall hear and dispose of the matter as in other civil cases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.471.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The supreme court may adopt rules which are consistent with this chapter and necessary or convenient to the impartial and speedy disposition of these cases.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.471.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.64.0">1953 c 755 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/686/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 686 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.121.0">1973 c 388 s 121</a>-124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/239/#laws.0.37.0">1976 c 239 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.128.0">1981 c 346 s 128</a>-131; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/247/#laws.0.72.0">1983 c 247 s 72</a>-74; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.152.0">1983 c 301 s 152</a>; 1986 c 444; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/110/#laws.1.3.0">2016 c 110 art 1 s 3</a>,4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.48"><b>176.48</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.481">
        <h1 class="shn">176.481 ORIGINAL JURISDICTION OF SUPREME COURT.</h1>
        <p>On review upon certiorari under this chapter, the supreme court has original jurisdiction. It may reverse, affirm, or modify the order allowing or disallowing compensation and enter such judgment as it deems just and proper. Where necessary the supreme court may remand the cause to the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals for a new hearing or for further proceedings with such directions as the court deems proper.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.481.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.65.0">1953 c 755 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.49"><b>176.49</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.491">
        <h1 class="shn">176.491 STAY OF PROCEEDINGS PENDING DISPOSITION OF CASE.</h1>
        <p>Where a writ of certiorari has been perfected under this chapter, it stays all proceedings for the enforcement of the order being reviewed until the case has been finally disposed of either in the supreme court or, where the cause has been remanded for a new hearing before a compensation judge or further proceedings before the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.491.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.66.0">1953 c 755 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.125.0">1973 c 388 s 125</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.132.0">1981 c 346 s 132</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.50"><b>176.50</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.501"><b>176.501</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.117.0">1987 c 332 s 117</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.51"><b>176.51</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COSTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.511">
        <h1 class="shn">176.511 COSTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.511.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Parties not awarded costs.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided otherwise by this chapter and specifically by this section, in appeals before the court of appeals or proceedings before the division or a compensation judge, costs shall not be awarded to any party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.511.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disbursements, taxation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner or compensation judge, or the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals on cases before the court, may award the prevailing party reimbursement for actual and necessary disbursements. Disbursements shall be taxed upon ten days' written notice to adverse parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.511.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Attorney fee, allowance.</span></h2>
          <p>Where (1) an award of compensation is affirmed, or modified and affirmed, (2) an order disallowing compensation is reversed, or (3) a petition to vacate an award is granted, the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals may include in its award an amount to cover a reasonable attorney fee or may allow an attorney fee in a proceeding to tax disbursements.</p>
          <p>If the employer or insurer files a notice of discontinuance of an employee's benefits and an administrative conference is held to resolve the dispute, but the employer or insurer fails to attend the administrative conference, the commissioner or compensation judge may order the employer or insurer to pay the employee's attorney fees as a cost under this section if the employee's benefits are continued.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.511.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Costs and disbursements on certiorari.</span></h2>
          <p>On review by the supreme court upon certiorari, costs and disbursements shall be taxed as they are upon appeals in civil actions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.511.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Attorney fee on certiorari.</span></h2>
          <p>Where upon a review by the supreme court upon certiorari, an award of compensation is affirmed, or modified and affirmed, or an order disallowing compensation is reversed, the court may allow a reasonable attorney fee incident to the review. This allowance of an attorney fee shall be made a part of the judgment order of the supreme court.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.176.511.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.68.0">1953 c 755 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/276/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 276 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/388/#laws.0.126.0">1973 c 388 s 126</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.19.0">1975 c 359 s 19</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/134/#laws.0.78.0">1976 c 134 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/342/#laws.0.22.0">1977 c 342 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/346/#laws.0.133.0">1981 c 346 s 133</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/234/#laws.0.16.0">1985 c 234 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/332/#laws.0.95.0">1987 c 332 s 95</a>-97; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/110/#laws.1.5.0">2016 c 110 art 1 s 5</a>,6</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.176.52"><b>176.52</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/755/#laws.0.83.0">1953 c 755 s 83</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SETTLEMENTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.176.521">
        <h1 class="shn">176.521 SETTLEMENT OF CLAIMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.521.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Validity.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An agreement between an employee or an employee's dependent and the employer or insurer to settle any claim for compensation under this chapter is valid where it has been executed in writing and signed by the parties and intervenors in the matter, and, where one or more of the parties is not represented by an attorney, the commissioner or a compensation judge has approved the settlement and made an award thereon. If the matter is upon appeal before the district court, the district court is the approving body. An agreement to settle any claim is not valid if a guardian or conservator is required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.092">176.092</a> and an employee or dependent has no guardian or conservator.</p>
          <p>(b) If the matter is on appeal before the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals, the proposed settlement shall be submitted for approval to a compensation judge at the Office of Administrative Hearings. Before the settlement is submitted to the compensation judge, the parties shall notify the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals and request that it suspend further action on the appeal pending review of the settlement by the compensation judge. Within 14 days after the compensation judge's final approval or disapproval of the settlement, the parties shall notify the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals of the compensation judge's action and shall request that the appeal be dismissed or reactivated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.521.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Approval.</span></h2>
          <p>Settlements shall be approved only if the terms conform with this chapter.</p>
          <p>The commissioner, a compensation judge, and the district court shall exercise discretion in approving or disapproving a proposed settlement.</p>
          <p>The parties to the agreement of settlement have the burden of proving that the settlement is reasonable, fair, and in conformity with this chapter. A settlement agreement where both the employee or the employee's dependent and the employer or insurer are represented by an attorney shall be conclusively presumed to be reasonable, fair, and in conformity with this chapter except when the settlement purports to be a full, final, and complete settlement of an employee's right to medical compensation under this chapter or rehabilitation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.102">176.102</a>. A settlement which purports to do so must be approved by the commissioner or a compensation judge.</p>
          <p>The conclusive presumption in this subdivision is not available in cases involving an employee or dependent with a guardian or conservator.</p>
          <p>The conclusive presumption in this subdivision applies to a settlement agreement entered into on or after January 15, 1982, whether the injury to which the settlement applies occurred prior to or on or after January 15, 1982.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.521.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Settlements not subject to approval.</span></h2>
          <p>When a settled case is not subject to approval, upon receipt of the stipulation for settlement, the commissioner or a compensation judge shall immediately sign the award and file it with the commissioner. Payment pursuant to the award shall be made within 14 days after it is filed with the commissioner. The commissioner may correct mathematical or clerical errors at any time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.176.521.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Partial settlement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The parties may file a partial stipulation for settlement which resolves the claims of the employee and reserves the claims of one or more intervenors. If the partial stipulation, or a letter of agreement attached to the partial stipulation, is not signed by an intervenor, the partial stipulation must include a statement that the parties were unable to:</p>
          <p>(1) obtain a response from the nonsigning intervenor regarding clarification or confirmation of its interest or an offer of settlement within a reasonable time despite good-faith efforts to obtain a response;</p>
          <p>(2) reach agreement with the nonsigning intervenor despite the belief that the parties negotiated with the intervenor in good faith and made a reasonable offer to settle the intervention claim; or</p>
